**High-level plan for final program**:
- Use a multi-start local optimizer on sequences of length N = maybe 1024 or 2048 (or 4096). Starting points:
   1. q (central binomial) for N.
   2. q with multiplicative random perturbations.
   3. Power-law profiles: a_i=(i+γ)^-α normalized, various α,γ, maybe scaled.
   4. Two-scale fractal / piecewise profiles.
   5. The height_sequence_1 if exists, and maybe downscaled/upsampled.
- Apply a custom `refine_sequence(a, time_budget)`:
   * Normalize to sum 1 (or max conv 1). Score F=2n max c.
   * Use mirror descent / gradient descent on log a with smoothed max, adaptive temperature.
   * Use block coordinate random perturbations (like previous but better) with line search, to escape.
   * Use "active-set equalization": identify lags with near-max, adjust weights for gradient, maybe use exact top-K.
   * Periodically try different temperatures/step schedules.
- Keep global best by exact-ish score (fast FFT; optionally exact np.convolve at end for lengths ≤20000? Evaluator uses np.convolve O(n^2). If final N=4096, exact okay. For internal, use FFT with small numerical error; final score maybe slightly under exact. We should return a sequence whose true evaluate (np.convolve) is ≤ claimed. Use conservative: at end, if n <= 20000, compute exact via np.convolve? Could be slow for 8192 (67M) but okay once. ```
import numpy as np, time, math
scipy? import scipy.signal, scipy.sparse, scipy.optimize.linprog? Use try except; if linprog unavailable, fallback.

global best_seq, best_score

def norm_max1(arr): arr=np.asarray; if all zero return; M=fast_max_conv(arr); arr/=M; return arr

def fast_convolution_fft(a):
   n=len(a); if n small exact? Use np.fft.
   m=1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,m)[:2*n-1]
   return np.maximum(c,0) maybe due tiny negative.

def score_fast(a): n=len; S=sum; c=fftconv(a); return 2*n*max(c)/S*S
```
For gradient, use c.

Mirror descent refine:
```
def refine_mirror(a, budget):
  a = a.copy()/sum; # sum1

Let's detail mirror descent.

Sum1 representation. Let p sum 1. Current objective `obj = max c` where c=conv(p,p). Score = 2n*obj.

Since p nonnegative length n and w_rev length 2n-1, output indices 0..3n-3; needed 0..2n-2. That's within. We can FFT length L >= 2n-1 maybe? **Fast convolution**: For n small (<128) use np.convolve exact; else FFT. But FFT error could affect score by ~1e-12. For internal choice okay. Use `np.asarray` float64. For convolution:
```
def convolve_nonneg_fft(a):
    n=a.size
    if n < 200: return np.convolve(a,a)
    m=1
    target=2*n-2
    while m < target: m <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,m)[:target+1]
    return np.maximum(c,0.0)
```
But for gradient, we need convolution with w where lengths differ; write separate `cross_corr_weights(p,w)`.

**Score functions**:
```
def score_from_conv(a, c):
    S=np.sum(a)
    if S<=1e-300: return inf
    M=np.max(c)
    return float(2*a.size*M/(S*S))
def score_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return inf
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000) # evaluator clamps
    c=conv(a)
    return score_from_conv(a,c)
```
Evaluator clamps negatives to0 and caps 1000. Our sequences positive ≤1 maybe. Use float64.

**q generation**:
```
def make_q(n, scale_to_max1=True):
    p=np.empty(n)
    v=1.0
    for i in range(n): p[i]=v; v *= (2*i+1)/(2*i+2)
    if scale_to_max1: c=conv(p); p/=max(c,eps)
    return p
```
For n up to 8192 loop okay. For larger, recurrence stable until i huge; v decays ~i^-1/2, fine.

def lp_iteration(f, M=1.0, time_limit=60):
    n=f.size
    A=build...
    res=linprog(np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M), bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'presolve':'highs', 'time_limit':time_limit})
    if res.success: return res.x
    return None
```

**Mirror descent detail**:
```
def refine_mirror(a, budget=300, verbose=False):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).copy()
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    S=a.sum(); if S<=0: return a
    p=a/S # sum1
    c=conv(p); best_mag=max(c); best_score=2*n*best_mag
    best=p.copy()
    deadline=time+ budget
    temps = [10,5,2,1,0.5,0.2,0.1,0.05]
    it=0
    while time<deadline:
       c=conv(p); M=max(c)
       if not finite: break
       # choose temp based on it
       tau = M* (temps[it % len] maybe decreasing)
       z=(c-M)/tau
       z=np.clip(z,-50,50)
       w=np.exp(z); w/=w.sum()
       h=cross_corr(p,w)
       g=2*h
       g=g-np.mean(g)
       # line search steps
       steps=[0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0]
       std=np.std(g)
       if std<=1e-15: continue
       best_step=None
       for step in steps:
          eta=step/std
          p_new=p*np.exp(-eta*g)
          p_new=np.clip(p_new,1e-300,None)
          p_new/=p_new.sum()
          c_new=conv(p_new); M_new=max(c_new)
          if M_new < best_mag -1e-12:
             accept...
             break
       if no accept, maybe random small step
       # random block perturbation
       if rng...
       it++
```
Need avoid evaluating too many candidates: Each p_new requires conv O(nlogn). n=4096, L=32768 FFT maybe 0.01s; 5 steps*100 iterations=500 conv ~5s. Good.

But gradient h computation requires another FFT of length 3n each iteration. For n=4096, L maybe 16384? cross length 3n → L=16384 for n=4096? 3n=12288, next 16384. Good. So each iteration two FFTs. Fine.

Cross-correlation FFT:
```
def cross_corr_p_w(p,w):
    n=p.size; Lw=len(w) #2n-1
    L=1; while L < n+Lw-1: L <<=1
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fw,L)
    idx = np.arange(2*n-2, n-1, -1) # [2n-2, 2n-3, ..., n-1]
    return c[idx]
```
Check index: For j=0 idx=2n-2; j=n-1 idx=n-1. The convolution output length L; values at idx needed. Good.
Could optimize by allocating idx each call? Precompute? Top-level no state? Can compute `indexes = np.arange(n-1, 2*n-2)[::-1]` in function; okay. Use `np.arange(n-1, 2*n-1)[::-1]`? For n=3: np.arange(2,6)[::-1]=[5,4,3] but we need [4,3,2]. Use `np.arange(2*n-2, n-2, -1)`: for n=3 start4 stop2 step-1 -> [4,3]? Missing2? Stop exclusive n-2=1? Actually np.arange(4,1,-1) = [4,3,2]. General need length n: start 2n-2, stop n-2 exclusive? For n=3 n-2=1 -> [4,3,2]; for n=4 start6 stop2 -> [6,5,4,3]; but need indices 6,5,4,3 (since 2n-2=6 down to n-1=3). Good. So `idx=np.arange(2*n-2, n-2, -1)`.

Wait output of conv p * w_rev: length (n-1)+(2n-2)=3n-3? indices 0..3n-3. Our L must be >=3n-2 (length). For n=4096 L=32768. OK.

Alternatively compute h via `np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(p,L) * np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],L), L)`.

def consider_sequence(a, label='', exact=False):
    global _BEST_SEQ, _BEST_SCORE
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(a,dtype=float).ravel()
    except: return
    if arr.size==0: return
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): return
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    arr=np.minimum(arr,1000.0)
    S=arr.sum()
    if S<=1e-12: return
    sc=score_fast(arr)
    if not np.isfinite(sc): return
    do_exact = exact or (arr.size <= 2048)
    if do_exact:
       try: sc_ex=evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist())
       except Exception: sc_ex=sc
       if np.isfinite(sc_ex): sc=sc_ex
    if sc < _BEST_SCORE - 1e-9:
       _BEST_SCORE = sc
       _BEST_SEQ = arr.copy()
       print(f"[{label}] n={arr.size} score={sc:.6f}")
    # store a few candidates for final exact if length<=12000
    if arr.size <= 12000 and len(_STORED_CANDS) < 50:
       # avoid too many? maybe store if score < best
```

**LP details in code**:
```
def lp_find_improvement(f, max_iters=20, time_limit_total=300):
    f=normalize_to_max1(f)
    start=time.monotonic()
    improved=False
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > start+time_limit_total: break
       res=lp_solve_one(f, timeout= min(30, remaining))
       if res is None: break
       g=np.clip(res,0,1000)
       # direct
       candidates=[]
       gmax=normalize_to_max1(g); candidates.append(gmax)
       sf=sum(f); sg=sum(g)
       if sg>0:
           gs=g*(sf/sg)
           for t in (0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0): candidates.append(f*(1-t)+gs*t)
       best_local=f
       best_sc=score_fast(f)
       for cand in candidates:
           if cand is None: continue
           cand=normalize_to_max1(cand)
           sc=score_fast(cand)
           if sc<best_sc-1e-10:
               best_sc=sc; best_local=cand
        if best_local is not f:
           f=best_local
           consider_sequence(f, label='LP')
           improved=True
           # update f for next LP
        else:
           # try small t 0.1? maybe
           if it>3: break
```
Direct LP may not improve due to noise; if no improvement for several iterations, break.

`lp_solve_one(f, timeout)`:
```
def lp_solve_one(f, timeout=60):
    n=f.size
    if not HAVE_SCIPY: return None
    if n > 1536: return None
    try:
       cols = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
       rows = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n) + np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
       data = np.tile(f, n)
       A=csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
       c=-np.ones(n)
       b=np.ones(2*n-1)
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'presolve':'highs', 'time_limit': timeout})
       if res.success or res.message in ('Optimal', ...): return res.x
    except MemoryError: return None
    return None
```
Wait if f max not exactly 1 due normalization, b should be max(f*f) maybe 1. If we normalized max1 via FFT, b=1. Good.
But if f has max1, constraints `f*g≤1`. For q direct g includes f feasible. If f0=1. Good.
However if f has max slightly <1 due normalization rounding, b=1 still loose. OK.
Could scale f so max=1 exactly by dividing by max c (using exact? fast). Good.

Potential issue: The matrix A can have values up to 1 and variables might need to be >1000; bounds inf. OK.

   if n not in dict:
      cols=... rows=...; dict[n]=(rows,cols)
   return
```
Then data=np.tile(f,n). This reduces allocation. For n=1024 template arrays length1M (24MB int64?) okay. But storing for multiple n memory. Clean after LP phases? Could `del _LP_TEMPLATES[n]` to free. Simpler no dict to avoid memory. But building rows/cols each iteration is heavy; maybe use dict and clear after each n.

def lp_solve_with_template(template, f, timeout):
   n=f.size; rows,cols=template; data=np.tile(f,n); A=csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
```

**Mirror descent implementation details**:
```
def mirror_refine(a, budget=60, seed=1):
    a=sum1 normalize
    best=a; best_mag=...; best_score=...
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline=time+ budget
    temps = [5.0,2.0,1.0,0.5,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02]
    it=0
    stuck=0
    while time < deadline:
       c=convolve_fast(p); M=max(c)
       tau = M * temps[min(it//10, len-1)]
       w=softmax(c, tau)
       h=cross_corr(p,w)
       g=2*(h - np.mean(h))
       std=np.std(g)
       accepted=False
       if std>0:
           for step in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0]:
               eta=step/std
               p_new=p*np.exp(-eta*g)
               p_new=np.clip(p_new,1e-300,None)
               ss=p_new.sum(); if ss<=0: continue
               p_new/=ss
               c_new=convolve_fast(p_new); M_new=max(c_new)
               if M_new < best_mag - 1e-10:
                   best_mag=M_new; best_score=...; best=p_new.copy(); p=p_new; accepted=True; break
           # If no accept, try median or less aggressive? maybe use negative step? No, only reduce.
       # Random block
       if not accepted and rng.random() < 0.5:
           # try a random block scale
           for _ in range(5):
              block len L = int(n * rng.uniform(0.01,0.5)); start...
              p2=p.copy()
              p2[start:start+L]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=L))
              sigma = 0.05 * (best_mag/(M+1e-300)+1) maybe
              normalize; if improve update
       it +=1
    return best
```
Gradient direction: We are minimizing weighted sum, but due nonconvex, reducing gradient can increase max elsewhere. Line search checks true max. Good.
Use `best_mag` (sum1 max conv). The objective score=2n*best_mag. Update global if improved.

Softmax: For c values, use `z = (c - M) / tau`; `z = np.clip(z, -50, 50)`; `w=np.exp(z); w/=w.sum()`. This weights max lags 1, lower lags <=1. For τ large, more uniform. Staring τ relative M: if M~0.75/n, tau=0.02M => z min ~ -? Many c tail maybe 0 => exp(-50) ignore. Good for later.

`cross_corr` each iteration expensive. We could recompute only if p changed? yes.

Line search steps: `eta=step/std`. If std very small, update near zero; skip.

The multiplicative update in sum1: We center g by mean before exp; because normalization removes global factor. This changes relative weights. `np.exp(-eta*(g-mean))` preserves sum approximately. If using `exp(-eta*g)`, normalization subtracts mean in a nonlinear way; centering is fine.

       num_tries maybe 100 per second

          start = int(rng.integers(0,n-block_len+1))
          sigma = current_sigma
          mult=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,block_len))
          p2=p.copy()
          p2[start:start+block_len]*=mult
          p2/=sum
          if score improve update p,best
       if no improve: sigma*=0.7; if sigma<0.005 restart sigma=0.1?

**Resampling**: If LP finds a profile at n=512 with high score, upsample to 8192 by interpolating in log-space. This could yield under 1.5 if asymptotic. Implement:
```
def resample_log(a, m):
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,a.size); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,m)
    loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
    logb=np.interp(x_new,x_old,loga)
    b=np.exp(logb)
    return b
```
If a has zeros, log floor; okay. Use linear in log. Could also direct interp for positive.

`convolve_fast`:
```
def convolve_fast(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n<=256:
       return np.convolve(a,a)
    L=_fft_len(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2*n-1]
    c[c<0]=0
    return c
```
For n large, np.convolve for n=256 direct maybe faster; use threshold 512.

`cross_corr_fast(p,w)`:
```
def cross_corr_fast(p,w):
    n=p.size; Lw=w.size
    need=n+Lw-1
    L=_fft_len(need)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,L)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fw,L)
    idx=np.arange(2*n-2, n-2, -1)
    return c[idx]
```

This family gives F≈π, worse. If cross sums avoid but c lower; no gain. q is one copy with R=1, F=π/2. Multiple copies length n? If R>1 but L=n/R, F=π? Worse. So copies bad.

What if use different scales (fractal) so cross intervals overlap but not add due to coefficients? Nonnegative add, bad.

Thus q/single endpoint singular may be strong. Yet target lower? Hmm.

Let's maybe consider **signed phase hidden in nonnegative via index permutation**. We can permute sequence indices arbitrarily; convolution with itself depends on sum diagonals. This is like arranging weights on [0,n-1] to minimize maximum weighted anti-diagonal sum. We can choose a **difference set** of weights. The q profile effectively weights all diagonals 0..n-1 equally via a dynamic allocation. Could there be a complete design where all 2n-1 diagonals are equal to C? That requires P^2 coefficients equal. We thought no polynomial with nonnegative coefficients degree n-1? Let's revisit with combinatorial number theory: Need a_i≥0 such that for all k=0..2n-2, D_k=∑_{i+j=k} a_i a_j = C. This is a sequence with constant autoconvolution. Such sequences are called **contour images**? For finite length, is it possible? If yes, F=1 exactly! Let's test small n=2: equations a0²=C,2a0a1=C,a1²=C -> a0=a1=√C, middle 2C=C => C=0. No positive. n=3? Maybe? Equations 5. Likely no. But near possible? The polynomial square root of constant polynomial has coefficients not all nonnegative. For constant all 2n-1 coefficients, P(z)=√(C(1-z^{2n-1})/(1-z)). For n=2, root 1+0.5z-0.125z²..., negative. For larger n, root maybe has negative coefficients. So exact flat impossible. But perhaps we can get max=C and sum larger via choosing first and last coefficients not all equal but factorization constraints.

This is a **moment problem**: find P≥0 with P² coefficients bounded. The optimal may be known as "minimum of L∞ norm of P^2 / P(1)^2" over nonnegative polynomials. This could be solved by Chebyshev polynomials? For nonnegative coefficients, perhaps the extremal is related to **Chebyshev polynomials of first kind**. Let's explore.

We want minimize max coefficient of P(z)^2 relative to P(1)^2. This is equivalent to: For polynomial P with positive coefficients, let L∞(P²) / P(1)^2. Since P(1) is L1 norm. There are inequalities from polynomial norm spaces. If we consider z on unit circle? Coefficient L∞ not same as sup norm. But maybe via root at 1. The flat target corresponds to P² approximating (1-z^{-?})? The optimal P might be the **Fejer kernel** spectral factor? Fejer kernel coefficients triangular; square? Hmm.

What if P(z) = 1/(1 - r z) truncated? We did geometric. What about P(z) = (1 + z + ... + z^{m-1}) / (1 + r z)? No.

Could use Chebyshev: The polynomial with positive coefficients and minimal max coefficient square for given P(1) might be derived from minimizing the maximum of a linear functional over a simplex; the optimum might have P_i following a **discrete solution to hyperbolic cosine profile**. For continuous, optimize ∫g with g*g≤1. This is an infinite-dimensional variational problem; the Euler-Lagrange equation for interior (where constraint active) may yield g*g = λ on a set, and λ*g? Let's derive for continuous: Maximize ∫g subject to g*g≤1, g≥0. KKT: 1 - ∫ λ(s) [g(s-x)+g(s?)]? If active set A, for x in support: ∫_A λ(s) (g(s-x)+? ) ds =1. Pseudo code for no lambda store:
```
def _store_candidate(arr, sc):
    global _STORED_CANDS
    if arr is None: return
    if len(_STORED_CANDS) < 25:
        _STORED_CANDS.append((sc, arr.copy()))
        return
    worst_idx=0; worst_sc=_STORED_CANDS[0][0]
    for i in range(1,len(_STORED_CANDS)):
        if _STORED_CANDS[i][0] > worst_sc:
            worst_sc=_STORED_CANDS[i][0]; worst_idx=i
    if sc < worst_sc:
        _STORED_CANDS[worst_idx]=(sc, arr.copy())
```

`consider_sequence` calls `_store_candidate` if finite.

def choose_exact_best(cands, budget):
    best_arr=None; best_sc=inf
    # sort top by score without lambda: create list of indices and sort key? Python sorted uses key; can avoid key by manual selection loop.
    # We'll just take up to 12 candidates: repeatedly find min score among remaining.
    remaining=list(cands)
    tried=[]
    while remaining and len(tried)<12 and time< budget:
       min_i=0; min_sc=remaining[0][0]
       for i in range(1,len(remaining)):
          if remaining[i][0] < min_sc: min_i=i
       sc0, arr = remaining.pop(min_i)
       if arr.size > 20000: continue
       try: sc=evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist())
       except: sc=sc0
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<best_sc:
          best_sc=sc; best_arr=arr.copy()
       tried.append((sc if finite else sc0, arr))
    return best_arr
```
Note using `np.isfinite(sc)` where sc might be float. If evaluate returns np.inf. Good.

def generate_candidates(n, seed):
   cands=[]
   cands.append(make_q(n, label='q')) # no label param needed
   for e in (0.0,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0): cands.append(make_power_decay(n,e))
   for r in (0.5,0.75,0.9,0.98): cands.append(make_geometric(n,r))
   # endpoint mix
   q=make_q(n); rev=q[::-1]
   for t in (0.2,0.5,0.8): cands.append((1-t)*q + t*rev)
   cands.append(np.exp(-np.linspace(0,2,n)))
   cands.append(np.exp(-np.linspace(2,0,n)))
   cands.append(np.ones(n))
   return cands
```

def make_power_decay(n, e):
   if abs(e)<1e-12: return np.ones(n)
   idx=np.arange(n)+1.0
   a=idx**(-e)
   return a
```

Implementation of `lp_find_improvement`:
```
def lp_find_improvement(f0, deadline):
    f = sanitize_sequence(f0)
    if f is None: return None
    # scale to max value 1 for numerical
    mx=np.max(f)
    if mx>0: f=f/mx
    M= max(convolve_fast(f))
    start=time.monotonic()
    template = None
    if HAVE_SCIPY:
       template = lp_template(f.size)
    no_improve=0
    for it in range(50):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       f = sanitize_sequence(f)
       mx=np.max(f); if mx>0: f=f/mx
       M=float(np.max(convolve_fast(f)))
       if not finite: break
       g=lp_solve(f, template, M, timeout=min(60, deadline-now))
       if g is None or np.sum(g)<=0: break
       candidates = [g]
       sf=sum(f); sg=sum(g)
       if sg>0:
          gs = g*(sf/sg)
          for t in (0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0): candidates.append(f*(1-t)+gs*t)
       # also random perturb of g
       cand_best=f; cand_sc=score_fast(f)
       for cand in candidates:
           a=sanitize_sequence(cand)
           if a is None: continue
           sc=score_fast(a)
           if sc < cand_sc - 1e-10:
              cand_sc=sc; cand_best=a
       if cand_best is not f:
           f=sanitize_sequence(cand_best)
           consider_sequence(f, label='LP')
           no_improve=0
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           if no_improve >= 6: break
    return f
```
Need `lp_solve` uses M in b. Template rows/cols based on n. If f size changes, template None.
```
def lp_solve(f, template, M, timeout):
   n=f.size
   if not HAVE_SCIPY or n>1536 or M<=0: return None
   try:
      if template is None:
         rows=...; cols=...
      else: rows,cols=template
      data=np.tile(f,n)
      A=csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
      c=-np.ones(n)
      b=np.full(2*n-1, M)
      res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'time_limit': timeout, 'presolve':'default'})
      if getattr(res,'success',False): return res.x
      # HiGHS status maybe 0 optimal; if res.x is not None and finite maybe use?
      if res.x is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
          # check constraints? accept
          return res.x
   except Exception:
      return None
```
If `linprog` times out, `res.message` may 'Terminated'? If res.x exists maybe not optimal but feasible? Could use if finite and constraints A@x ≤ b(1+1e-6). But multiplying A@x for n=1024 dense? Could use `A.dot(res.x)` sparse, okay. Check.
```
if res.x is not None:
   val=A.dot(res.x)
   if np.all(val <= M*(1+1e-6)+1e-8): return np.maximum(res.x,0)
```
But A.dot can allocate length 2n. OK.

Template building:

def lp_template(n):
   if n>1536: return None
   cols=np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)
   rows=np.repeat(np.arange(n), n) + np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
   return (rows, cols)
```

```

def softmax_weights(c, tau):
    c=c - np.max(c)
    z=np.clip(c/tau, -50, 50)
    w=np.exp(z); s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: w=np.full(len(c),1/len(c))
    else: w/=s
    return w
```

**Block refine**:
```
def block_refine(a, deadline, seed=1):
    p=a/a.sum(); best=p; best_mag=...
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    sigma=0.2; block_frac=0.1
    while time<deadline:
       improved=False
       for _ in range(50):
          L=int(n*rng.uniform(0.005,0.5)); ...
          mult=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,L))
          p2=p.copy(); p2[start:start+L]*=mult; p2/=sum
          M2=max(conv(p2))
          if M2<best_mag-1e-10: update p,best, improved=True
       if not improved: sigma*=0.8; if sigma<0.01: sigma=0.05
    return best
```
Could be slow for n=8192 doing 50 conv per loop; each conv 32768 FFT ~0.005s? 0.25s per loop. Many loops. Use 10 candidates per loop. Set outer loop until time.

`add_resampled`:
```
def resample_log(a, m):
    if a.size == m: return a.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
    logb=np.interp(x_new,x_old,loga)
    b=np.exp(logb)
    return b
```
For large a with many tiny values below floor, tail may be overfilled. But okay.

Maybe direct linear interpolation of values better for q? q decays; log is good. If small values 1e-? q at n=1024 last ~0.017, not tiny. ok.

**Scoring q exactly**: q score for n=8192 maybe 1.571. If no LP/mirror, final q. Good.

Let's ensure `lp_find_improvement` uses `convolve_fast(f)` after scaling max value1. If f has f0=0 but positive elsewhere, row0 constraint none; M could be at later. LP might choose g0 huge? But direct score checks. Starting sequences have positive first element (q, power). Good.

`lp_template` for n as int. In `lp_find_improvement`, if f.size not same? f size fixed. Build template once.

Potential issue: `linprog` with sparse A and `b` maybe small; if all b 0 (M=0) returns zero. We check M.

**Time in top exact select**: It may use `evaluate_sequence` which is slow; set top limit 5 and only if time >2. For each, if `arr.size > 10000` skip to avoid too slow? q length 8192 okay. Set max exact len 12000. Good.

**Print in exact select**: maybe.

Now final answer. Ensure code block only. Need no analysis in final. Use desired oververbosity final code only. Let's produce.
</think>

```python
"""
Search non-negative height sequences minimizing

    evaluate_sequence(a) = 2*n*max(a*a) / (sum(a)^2),

where a*a is NumPy full convolution.

Algorithm:
- Use several analytic initial profiles, in particular the central-binomial
  profile whose infinite self-convolution is flat.
- Run an alternating LP-improvement pass inspired by the previous state of
  the art: for fixed f, solve max sum(g) subject to f*g <= max(f*f).
- Polish candidates with FFT-based mirror descent on a smoothed maximum and
  adaptive random block perturbations.
- Keep the best sequence over several lengths and perform final official
  evaluations on a shortlist.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

_BEST_SEQ = None
_BEST_SCORE = float("inf")
_STORED_CANDS = []


def _reset_globals():
    global _BEST_SEQ, _BEST_SCORE, _STORED_CANDS
    _BEST_SEQ = None
    _BEST_SCORE = float("inf")
    _STORED_CANDS = []


def _sanitize_sequence(a):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    mx = float(np.max(arr)) if arr.size else 0.0
    if mx > 1000.0:
        arr *= (1000.0 / mx)
    elif mx > 0.0:
        pass
    arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
    if float(np.sum(arr)) <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return arr


def _store_candidate(arr, sc):
    global _STORED_CANDS
    if arr is None or not np.isfinite(sc):
        return
    if arr.size > 16000:
        return
    if len(_STORED_CANDS) < 25:
        _STORED_CANDS.append((float(sc), arr.copy()))
        return
    worst_idx = 0
    worst_sc = _STORED_CANDS[0][0]
    for i in range(1, len(_STORED_CANDS)):
        if _STORED_CANDS[i][0] > worst_sc:
            worst_sc = _STORED_CANDS[i][0]
            worst_idx = i
    if float(sc) < worst_sc:
        _STORED_CANDS[worst_idx] = (float(sc), arr.copy())


def _consider_sequence(a, label="cand", exact=False):
    global _BEST_SEQ, _BEST_SCORE
    arr = _sanitize_sequence(a)
    if arr is None:
        return
    sc = _score_fast(arr)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return
    do_exact = exact
    if not do_exact and arr.size <= 2048:
        do_exact = True
    if do_exact:
        try:
            sc_exact = evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist())
            if np.isfinite(sc_exact):
                sc = float(sc_exact)
        except Exception:
            pass
    if sc < _BEST_SCORE - 1e-10:
        _BEST_SCORE = float(sc)
        _BEST_SEQ = arr.copy()
        print("[score] label={} n={} score={:.7f}".format(label, arr.size, sc), flush=True)
    _store_candidate(arr, sc)


def _fft_len(need):
    need = int(max(1, need))
    if need <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (need - 1).bit_length()


def _convolve_fast(a):
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    if n <= 256:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        L = _fft_len(2 * n - 1)
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[:2 * n - 1]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float)
    c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c


def _score_fast(a):
    arr = _sanitize_sequence(a)
    if arr is None:
        return float("inf")
    n = arr.size
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return float("inf")
    c = _convolve_fast(arr)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def _score_mag_sum1(p):
    n = int(p.size)
    c = _convolve_fast(np.asarray(p, dtype=float))
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def _make_q(n):
    n = int(n)
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
    v = 1.0
    for i in range(n):
        a[i] = v
        v *= (2.0 * i + 1.0) / (2.0 * i + 2.0)
    return a


def _make_power_decay(n, e):
    n = int(n)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=float) + 1.0
    if abs(e) < 1e-12:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    return idx ** (-e)


def _make_geometric(n, r):
    n = int(n)
    r = float(r)
    if r <= 0.0:
        a = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
        a[0] = 1.0
        return a
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    return np.exp(x * math.log(r))


def _generate_initial_candidates(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return []
    cands = []
    cands.append(_make_q(n))
    for e in (0.25, 0.4, 0.5, 0.65, 0.8):
        cands.append(_make_power_decay(n, e))
    cands.append(_make_power_decay(n, 0.0))
    for r in (0.6, 0.8, 0.92, 0.985):
        cands.append(_make_geometric(n, r))
    q = _make_q(n)
    rev = q[::-1]
    for t in (0.15, 0.4, 0.7):
        cands.append((1.0 - t) * q + t * rev)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    cands.append(np.exp(-2.2 * x))
    cands.append(np.exp(-2.2 * (1.0 - x)))
    cands.append(1.0 / np.sqrt(1.0 + np.arange(n, dtype=float) * 0.001))
    return cands


def _best_for_length(n):
    n = int(n)
    best = None
    best_sc = float("inf")
    for c in _generate_initial_candidates(n):
        a = _sanitize_sequence(c)
        if a is None:
            continue
        sc = _score_fast(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best = a
    if best is None:
        best = _make_q(n)
        best_sc = _score_fast(best)
    _consider_sequence(best, label="init-n{}".format(n))
    return best


def _resample_log(a, m):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).ravel()
    if a.size == int(m):
        return a.copy()
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(m))
    ya = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    yb = np.interp(x1, x0, ya)
    return np.exp(yb)


def _softmax_weights(c, tau):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float)
    if c.size == 0:
        return c.copy()
    mmax = float(np.max(c))
    if tau <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(tau):
        tau = 1e-6
    z = (c - mmax) / tau
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 50.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.full(c.size, 1.0 / c.size)
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def _cross_corr_p_w(p, w):
    n = int(p.size)
    lw = int(w.size)
    idx = np.arange(2 * n - 2, n - 2, -1, dtype=int)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=float)
    if n * lw <= 300000:
        tmp = np.convolve(p, w[::-1])
        out = tmp[idx]
        out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
        return out
    need = n + lw - 1
    L = _fft_len(need)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], L)
    tmp = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, L)
    out = tmp[idx]
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def _mirror_refine(a, deadline, seed=7):
    a = _sanitize_sequence(a)
    if a is None:
        return None
    p = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).copy()
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    p /= s
    n = int(p.size)
    best = p.copy()
    best_mag = float(np.max(_convolve_fast(p)))
    if not np.isfinite(best_mag):
        return None
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    temps = (5.0, 2.5, 1.5, 1.0, 0.6, 0.35, 0.2, 0.12, 0.07, 0.04)
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < max(deadline - 2.0, 0.0):
        c = _convolve_fast(p)
        M = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break
        temp = temps[it % len(temps)]
        w = _softmax_weights(c, temp * M)
        h = _cross_corr_p_w(p, w)
        if h.size != n:
            break
        g = 2.0 * float(h)
        g -= float(np.mean(g))
        scale = float(np.std(g))
        if scale < 1e-14:
            scale = max(float(np.max(np.abs(g))), 1e-12)
        accepted = False
        for step in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35, 0.6, 1.0):
            eta = step / scale
            e = -eta * g
            e = np.clip(e, -12.0, 12.0)
            pn = p * np.exp(e)
            sn = float(np.sum(pn))
            if sn <= 1e-300:
                continue
            pn /= sn
            cn = _convolve_fast(pn)
            Mn = float(np.max(cn))
            if np.isfinite(Mn) and Mn < best_mag - 1e-12:
                best_mag = Mn
                best = pn.copy()
                p = pn.copy()
                accepted = True
                break
        if not accepted and rng.random() < 0.35:
            for _ in range(5):
                frac = float(rng.uniform(0.005, 0.45))
                L = max(1, int(n * frac))
                start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - L + 1)))
                stop = min(n, start + L)
                sigma = 0.08 + 0.25 * float(rng.random())
                pn = p.copy()
                pn[start:stop] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=(stop - start)))
                sn = float(np.sum(pn))
                if sn <= 1e-300:
                    continue
                pn /= sn
                cn = _convolve_fast(pn)
                Mn = float(np.max(cn))
                if np.isfinite(Mn) and Mn < best_mag - 1e-12:
                    best_mag = Mn
                    best = pn.copy()
                    p = pn.copy()
                    accepted = True
                    break
        it += 1
        if it % 40 == 0:
            print("  mirror n={} mag={:.8f} score_mag={:.7f}".format(
                n, best_mag, 2.0 * n * best_mag), flush=True)
    return best


def _block_refine(a, deadline, seed=11):
    a = _sanitize_sequence(a)
    if a is None:
        return None
    p = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).copy()
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return None
    p /= s
    n = int(p.size)
    best = p.copy()
    best_mag = float(np.max(_convolve_fast(p)))
    if not np.isfinite(best_mag):
        return None
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    sigma = 0.25
    while time.monotonic() < max(deadline - 2.0, 0.0):
        improved = False
        for _ in range(10):
            frac = float(rng.uniform(0.003, 0.55))
            L = max(1, int(n * frac))
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - L + 1)))
            stop = min(n, start + L)
            pn = p.copy()
            pn[start:stop] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=(stop - start)))
            sn = float(np.sum(pn))
            if sn <= 1e-300:
                continue
            pn /= sn
            cn = _convolve_fast(pn)
            Mn = float(np.max(cn))
            if np.isfinite(Mn) and Mn < best_mag - 1e-12:
                best_mag = Mn
                best = pn.copy()
                p = pn.copy()
                improved = True
        if not improved:
            sigma *= 0.78
            if sigma < 0.015:
                sigma = 0.18
    return best


def _lp_template(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n < 1 or n > 1536:
        return None
    cols = np.repeat(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)
    rows = (np.repeat(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n) +
            np.tile(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)).astype(np.int32)
    return (rows, cols)


def _lp_solve(f, template, M, timeout):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    n = int(f.size)
    if n < 2 or n > 1536 or M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None
    try:
        if template is None:
            return None
        rows, cols = template
        data = np.tile(np.asarray(f, dtype=float), n)
        A = csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n))
        c = -np.ones(n, dtype=float)
        b = np.full(2 * n - 1, M, dtype=float)
        try:
            res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, None),
                          method="highs",
                          options={"presolve": "default", "time_limit": max(5.0, float(timeout))})
        except Exception:
            res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0.0, None), method="highs")
        x = getattr(res, "x", None)
        if x is None:
            return None
        x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
        if x.size != n:
            return None
        x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
            return None
        if True:
            val = np.asarray(A.dot(x)).ravel()
            if val.size != 2 * n - 1:
                val = _convolve_fast(f)  # fallback check not used for x; keep finite
        success = getattr(res, "success", False)
        if not success:
            # Accept a finite feasible-looking solution from HiGHS if available.
            val = np.asarray(A.dot(x)).ravel()
            if val.size == 2 * n - 1 and np.all(val <= M * (1.0 + 1e-6) + 1e-9):
                pass
            else:
                return None
        return x
    except Exception:
        return None


def _lp_find_improvement(f0, deadline, label="LP"):
    f = _sanitize_sequence(f0)
    if f is None:
        return None
    template = _lp_template(f.size)
    no_improve = 0
    for it in range(80):
        if time.monotonic() > max(deadline - 3.0, 0.0):
            break
        f = _sanitize_sequence(f)
        if f is None:
            break
        mx = float(np.max(f))
        if mx > 0.0:
            f = f / mx
        M = float(np.max(_convolve_fast(f)))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break
        remain = max(5.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic())
        timeout = min(90.0, max(12.0, remain * 0.35))
        g = _lp_solve(f, template, M, timeout)
        if g is None:
            break
        sg = float(np.sum(g))
        sf = float(np.sum(f))
        candidates = [g]
        if sg > 0.0 and sf > 0.0:
            gs = g * (sf / sg)
            for t in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0):
                candidates.append(f * (1.0 - t) + gs * t)
        best = f
        best_sc = _score_fast(f)
        for cand in candidates:
            a = _sanitize_sequence(cand)
            if a is None:
                continue
            sc = _score_fast(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-9:
                best_sc = sc
                best = a
        if best is not f:
            f = _sanitize_sequence(best)
            _consider_sequence(f, label=label + "/{}".format(it))
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve >= 6:
                break
        if it % 8 == 0:
            print("  LP iter {} n={} score={:.7f}".format(it, f.size, best_sc), flush=True)
    return f


def _select_exact_best(candidates, deadline):
    best_arr = None
    best_sc = float("inf")
    remaining = list(candidates)
    tried = 0
    max_trials = 6
    while remaining and tried < max_trials and time.monotonic() < max(deadline - 2.0, 0.0):
        min_i = 0
        min_sc = remaining[0][0]
        for i in range(1, len(remaining)):
            if remaining[i][0] < min_sc:
                min_sc = remaining[i][0]
                min_i = i
        _, arr = remaining.pop(min_i)
        tried += 1
        arr = _sanitize_sequence(arr)
        if arr is None:
            continue
        if arr.size > 12000:
            continue
        try:
            sc = evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist())
        except Exception:
            sc = float("inf")
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            continue
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best_arr = arr.copy()
            print("[exact-shortlist] n={} score={:.7f}".format(arr.size, sc), flush=True)
    return best_arr


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    global _BEST_SEQ, _BEST_SCORE, _STORED_CANDS
    _reset_globals()

    budget_s = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + budget_s - 5.0

    try:
        parent = height_sequence_1
        if parent is not None:
            _consider_sequence(parent, label="parent")
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Initial profiles at several lengths.
    best_by_len = {}
    small_lengths = (256, 512, 1024)
    for n in small_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 600.0:
            break
        if n not in best_by_len:
            best_by_len[n] = _best_for_length(n)

    # Get a decent large-length start too, but do not spend too long here.
    large_lengths = (2048, 4096, 8192)
    for n in large_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 300.0:
            break
        best_by_len[n] = _best_for_length(n)

    # Choose the best small-profile for LP and for upsampling.
    best_small = None
    best_small_score = float("inf")
    for n in small_lengths:
        a = best_by_len.get(n)
        if a is None:
            continue
        sc = _score_fast(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_small_score:
            best_small_score = sc
            best_small = a

    # LP alternation on the most promising small/moderate lengths.
    if HAVE_SCIPY:
        lp_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + min(1100.0, 0.42 * (deadline - time.monotonic())))
        for n in (512, 1024):
            f = best_by_len.get(n)
            if f is None:
                continue
            before = time.monotonic()
            f2 = _lp_find_improvement(f, lp_deadline, label="LP-{}".format(n))
            if f2 is not None:
                sc2 = _score_fast(f2)
                if np.isfinite(sc2):
                    best_by_len[n] = f2
                print("[LP-phase] n={} elapsed={:.1f}s best_score={:.7f}".format(
                    n, time.monotonic() - before, sc2), flush=True)
            if time.monotonic() > lp_deadline - 30.0:
                break

    # Reidentify best small profile after LP.
    best_small = None
    best_small_score = float("inf")
    for n in small_lengths:
        a = best_by_len.get(n)
        if a is None:
            continue
        sc = _score_fast(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_small_score:
            best_small_score = sc
            best_small = a

    # Mirror / block polishing on larger lengths.
    # We use multiple starts: q at that length and a log-resampled small best.
    seq_no = 0
    for n in large_lengths:
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 120.0:
            break
        qn = _make_q(n)
        starts = [qn]
        if best_small is not None:
            starts.append(_resample_log(best_small, n))
        a_large = best_by_len.get(n)
        if a_large is not None:
            starts.append(a_large)

        for start in starts:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60.0:
                break
            start = _sanitize_sequence(start)
            if start is None:
                continue
            seq_no += 1
            sub_dead = min(deadline - 60.0, time.monotonic() + max(90.0, remaining / 3.0))
            pm = _mirror_refine(start, sub_dead, seed=1000 + seq_no)
            if pm is not None:
                _consider_sequence(pm, label="mirror-{}-{}".format(n, seq_no), exact=False)
                sub_dead2 = min(deadline - 60.0, time.monotonic() + 70.0)
                pb = _block_refine(pm, sub_dead2, seed=2000 + seq_no)
                if pb is not None:
                    _consider_sequence(pb, label="blocks-{}-{}".format(n, seq_no), exact=False)
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120.0:
                break

    # Final shortlist exact selection.
    final_arr = None
    if _BEST_SEQ is not None:
        final_arr = _BEST_SEQ
    if _STORED_CANDS:
        exact_choice = _select_exact_best(_STORED_CANDS, deadline)
        if exact_choice is not None:
            final_arr = exact_choice
    if final_arr is None:
        final_arr = best_by_len.get(1024)
        if final_arr is None:
            final_arr = _make_q(1024)

    final_arr = _sanitize_sequence(final_arr)
    if final_arr is None:
        final_arr = _make_q(1024)
        final_arr = _sanitize_sequence(final_arr)
        if final_arr is None:
            final_arr = np.ones(1024, dtype=float)

    # Final scoring sanity.
    try:
        final_score = evaluate_sequence(final_arr.tolist())
        if np.isnotfinite(final_score):
            final_score = _score_fast(final_arr)
        print("[final] n={} score={:.7f}".format(final_arr.size, final_score), flush=True)
    except Exception:
        print("[final] n={} fast_score={:.7f}".format(final_arr.size, _score_fast(final_arr)), flush=True)

    return [float(x) for x in final_arr]
```
